\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}x^{\top}_{i}}{n}\right)^{-1}(M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu))/\sigma

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}x^{\top}_{i}}{n}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}x^{\top}_{i}}{n}-I_{d}\right)M(\theta,\nu)/\sigma

By using Operator norm bounds for the standard Gaussian ensemble in Lemma of Subsection
E.3
, and
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}

\left\|\left(\frac{\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}x^{\top}_{i}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)\qquad\left\|\frac{\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}x^{\top}_{i}}{n}-I_{d}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

By invoking the proven upper bound of Statistical Error of Regression
Parameters for Easy EM, and using the facts that
\|M(\theta,\nu)\|\leq\|\theta\|
is nonincreasing and bounded
\|M(\theta,\nu)\|/\sigma=\mathcal{O}(1)
in Subsection
B.2
, the bounds in this proposition are established.
∎

Appendix G
Proofs for Results of Finite-Sample Level Analysis

G.1
Proof for Initialization at Finite-Sample Level

G.1
.

(Fact
6.2
in Section
6
: Initialization at Finite-Sample Level)

Let
\alpha^{t}:=\|\theta^{t}\|/\sigma=\|M_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})\|/\sigma
and
\beta^{t}:=\tanh(\nu^{t})=N_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})
for all
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
be the
t
-th iteration of the EM update rules
\|M_{n}(\theta,\nu)\|/\sigma
and
N_{n}(\theta,\nu)
at the finite-sample level. If we run EM at
the finite-sample level for at most
T_{0}=\mathcal{O}(1)
iterations with
n=\Omega\left(d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
samples, then
\alpha^{T_{0}}<0.1
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

For brevity, we write
\bar{\alpha}^{t}:=\|M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})\|/\sigma
for the EM update rule at population level. By invoking the
bound for the statistical error of regression parameters in Subsection
F.4
, and
selecting
n\geq(2000C)^{2}\left[d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]

for some constant
C

|\alpha^{t}-\bar{\alpha}^{t}|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}

If
\alpha^{t}<0.1
, then it satisfies the condition;
otherwise, by invoking the population EM update rule
\bar{\alpha}^{t+1}=m(\alpha^{t},\nu^{t})=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X]
in equation
11
,
and the monotonicity of
m(\alpha,\nu)
in Fact
4.2
of Section
4
,

\alpha^{t+1}\leq\bar{\alpha}^{t+1}+|\alpha^{t+1}-\bar{\alpha}^{t+1}|=m(\alpha^{t},\nu^{t})+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}\leq m(\alpha^{t},0)+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}=m_{0}(\alpha^{t})+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}

Applying the monotonicity of
m(\alpha,\nu)
in Fact
4.2
of Section
4
, which is also applicable to
m_{0}(\alpha)=m(\alpha,0)

\displaystyle\alpha^{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle m_{0}(\alpha^{0})+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}\leq m_{0}(\infty)+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}=\frac{2}{\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}<0.64

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle m_{0}(\alpha^{1})+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}\leq m_{0}\left(0.64\right)+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}<0.4

\displaystyle\alpha^{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle m_{0}(\alpha^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}\leq m_{0}\left(0.4\right)+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}<0.31

Furthermore, by applying the upper bound for expectation
m_{0}(\alpha)=m(\alpha,0)=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X)X]
under the density function
X\sim K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
in Subsection
A.3
for
t\geq 3

\alpha^{t+1}\leq m_{0}(\alpha^{t})+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}\leq\alpha^{t}-\frac{3[\alpha^{t}]^{3}}{1+8[\alpha^{t}]}+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}

By using
\alpha^{t}\geq 0.1
, then
r^{t}:=\alpha^{t}-\frac{1}{20}\geq\frac{1}{20}
and

r^{t+1}\leq r^{t}-\frac{5}{3}\left[r^{t}+\frac{1}{20}\right]^{3}+\frac{1}{2}\times 10^{-3}<r^{t}-\frac{5}{3}[r^{t}]^{3}<r^{t}-[r^{t}]^{3}

Hence, by using
0<r^{t+1}\leq r^{t}
, we obtain
2\leq\left(\frac{r^{t}}{r^{t+1}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{r^{t}}{r^{t+1}}\right)

\displaystyle 2(T_{0}-3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=3}^{T_{0}-1}\frac{r^{t}-r^{t+1}}{\frac{1}{2}[r^{t}]^{3}}\leq\sum_{t=3}^{T_{0}-1}\left\{[r^{t}]^{-3}\left[\left(\frac{r^{t}}{r^{t+1}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{r^{t}}{r^{t+1}}\right)\right]\right\}\{r^{t}-r^{t+1}\}=[r^{T_{0}}]^{-2}-[r^{3}]^{-2}

By selecting
T_{0}=196=\mathcal{O}(1)
and using
r^{3}:=\alpha^{3}-\frac{1}{20}<0.31-\frac{1}{20}
, we have

\alpha^{T_{0}}=r^{T_{0}}+\frac{1}{20}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2(T_{0}-3)+[r^{3}]^{-2}}}+\frac{1}{20}<\frac{1}{20}+\frac{1}{20}=0.1

By substituting
\delta/T_{0}\rightarrow\delta
in the above expressions,
then
\alpha^{T_{0}}<0.1
with probability at least
1-\delta
.
∎

G.2
Proof for Convergence Rate at Finite-Sample Level

G.2
.

(Theorem
6.1
in Section
6
: Convergence Rate at Finite-Sample Level for Fixed Mixing Weights)
Suppose a 2MLR model is fitted to the overspecified
model with no separation
\theta^{\ast}=\vec{0}
, given mixing weights
\pi^{t}=\pi^{0}

and
n=\Omega\left(d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\vee\log^{3}\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)
samples:

(sufficiently unbalanced) if
\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\gtrsim\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
finite-sample EM takes at most
T=\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|^{-2}_{1}\log\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)
iterations to achieve
\|\theta^{T}\|/\sigma=\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|^{-1}_{1}\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
,

(sufficiently balanced) if
\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
finite-sample EM takes at most
T=\mathcal{O}\left(\left[\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
iterations to achieve
\|\theta^{T}\|/\sigma=\mathcal{O}\left(\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)
,
with probability at least
1-T(\delta+\delta^{\prime})
.

Proof.

By invoking the fact for initialization
at finite-sample level in Subsection
G.1
, then
\alpha^{T_{0}}=\|\theta^{T_{0}}\|/\sigma<0.1
after running population EM for at most
T_{0}=\mathcal{O}(1)
iterations. Without loss of generality, we may assume
\alpha^{0}=\|\theta^{0}\|/\sigma\in(0,0.1)
in the following discussions.

For brevity, we write the output of the EM update rule for regression
parameters at Population level as
\bar{\alpha}^{t+1}=\|M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})\|/\sigma=\|M(\theta^{t},\nu^{0})\|/\sigma
,
and output of the EM update rules for regression parameters at Finite-smaple level as

\alpha^{t+1}=\|\theta^{t+1}\|/\sigma=\|M_{n}(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})\|/\sigma=\|M_{n}(\theta^{t},\nu^{0})\|/\sigma
.

Without the loss of generality, we assume
\beta^{t}=\pi^{t}(1)-\pi^{t}(2)\geq 0
, then by invoking the inequality in Appendix
C
for
\alpha^{t}\leq 0.1

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\alpha}^{t+1}}{\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1-\frac{5}{3}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}+9.53[\alpha^{t}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2}

and the upper bound for statistical error in Subsection
F.4
, namely
|\alpha^{t+1}-\bar{\alpha}^{t+1}|\leq c^{2}(\beta^{t}+\alpha^{t})\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
for some
univeral constant
c
when
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\vee\log^{3}\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\alpha^{t+1}-\bar{\alpha}^{t+1}|+\bar{\alpha}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\left(1-\frac{5}{3}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}+9.53[\alpha^{t}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2}\right)+c^{2}(\beta^{t}+\alpha^{t})\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{t}\left(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}+c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)+c^{2}\beta^{t}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}-[\alpha^{t}]^{3}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\left(\frac{5}{3}-9.53[\beta^{t}]^{2}\right)

with the probability at least
1-(\delta+\delta^{\prime})
.

Proof for part a) (sufficiently unbalanced)
\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\gtrsim\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}

Consider the following two cases.

(i) if
|\beta^{t}|=|\beta^{0}|=\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\geq 2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
|\beta^{t}|>0.4
, by invoking
-(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\left(\frac{5}{3}-9.53[\beta^{t}]^{2}\right)<\frac{5}{3},|\beta^{t}|\leq 1

\displaystyle\alpha^{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha^{t}\left(1-0.4^{2}+c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\frac{5}{3}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}\right)+c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

By selecting
n\geq(5c)^{4}\left[d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]
,
then
1-0.4^{2}+c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\frac{5}{3}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}<0.9
for
\alpha^{t}\leq 0.1
, therefore

\alpha^{t+1}-10c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\leq 0.9\left(\alpha^{t}-10c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

Furthermore, by letting
n\geq(10c)^{4}\left[d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]
, then
10c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\leq 0.1
and
\alpha^{t+1}\leq 0.1
, hence

\alpha^{T}\leq 10c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+0.9^{T}\left(\alpha^{0}-10c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)\leq 10c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+0.1\times 0.9^{T}

Let’s choose
T=\lceil\frac{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}-2\log c-\log 10}{\log\frac{1}{0.9}}\rceil_{+}=\Theta\left(\log\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)
, then

\alpha^{T}\leq 10c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}=11c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}=\Theta\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

(ii) if
|\beta^{t}|=|\beta^{0}|=\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\geq 2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
|\beta^{t}|\leq 0.4
, by invoking
-(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\left(\frac{5}{3}-9.53[\beta^{t}]^{2}\right)<-0.1,|\beta^{t}|\leq 0.4

\alpha^{t+1}\leq\alpha^{t}\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\beta^{t}]^{2}\right)+c^{2}\beta^{t}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}-0.1[\alpha^{t}]^{3}

Hence,

\alpha^{t+1}-\frac{4}{3}\frac{c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\leq\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\beta^{0}]^{2}\right)\left(\alpha^{t}-\frac{4}{3}\frac{c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

Furthermore, by letting
n\geq\left(\frac{20}{3}c\right)^{4}\left[d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]
, then
\frac{4}{3}\frac{c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\leq\frac{2}{3}c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq 0.1

\alpha^{T}\leq\frac{4}{3}\frac{c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+0.1\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\beta^{0}]^{2}\right)^{T}

Let’s choose
T=\lceil\frac{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}-\log\frac{1}{\beta^{0}}+\log\frac{3}{2c^{2}}}{-\log\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\beta^{0}]^{2}\right)}\rceil_{+}=\Theta\left([\beta^{0}]^{-2}\log\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)
for
|\beta^{0}|\leq 0.4
, here we use that fact
-\log(1-\frac{3}{4}[\beta^{0}]^{2})=\Theta([\beta^{0}]^{2})
for small
\beta^{0}
, then

\alpha^{T}\leq\frac{4}{3}\frac{c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}=\frac{2c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

To sum up, by combining case (i) and case (ii), we show that with
n=\Omega\left(d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\vee\log^{3}\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)
and
|\beta^{0}|=\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\geq 2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}},\alpha^{0}=\|\theta^{0}\|/\sigma\leq 0.1
, if we run finite-sample EM for
at most
T=\mathcal{O}\left([\beta^{0}]^{-2}\log\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)
iterations, then
\alpha^{T}=\|\theta^{T}\|/\sigma=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)
with probability at least
1-T(\delta+\delta^{\prime})
.

Proof for part b) (sufficiently balanced)
\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}

If
|\beta^{t}|=|\beta^{0}|=\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\leq 2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
let
n\geq(10c)^{4}\left[d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]
, then

\beta^{t}\leq\frac{1}{5},1+\frac{1}{4(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}<\frac{5}{3}-9.53[\beta^{t}]^{2}
,

\alpha^{t+1}\leq\alpha^{t}\left(c^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}-\frac{[\alpha^{t}]^{2}}{4}+(1-[\alpha^{t}]^{2})(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\right)+c^{2}\beta^{t}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

If
\alpha^{t}\leq 2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}
, then the goal
\alpha^{t}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)

is achieved; otherwise, we have
\alpha^{t}>2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}
,

\alpha^{t+1}\leq(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\alpha^{t}(1-[\alpha^{t}]^{2})+c^{2}\beta^{t}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

(i) if
\alpha^{t}>\frac{2c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
, note that
\alpha^{t}(1-[\alpha^{t}]^{2})\geq\frac{\alpha^{t}}{2}
for
\alpha^{t}\leq 0.1<\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}
, then

\alpha^{t+1}\leq\alpha^{t}(1-[\alpha^{t}]^{2})

(ii) if
\alpha^{t}\leq\frac{2c^{2}}{\beta^{0}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
, then by invoking
1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}\leq 1
and

\alpha^{t}>2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}
, we have

\alpha^{t+1}\leq\alpha^{t}(1-[\alpha^{t}]^{2})+\frac{2c^{4}}{\alpha^{t}}\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\leq\alpha^{t}(1-[\alpha^{t}]^{2})+2c^{3}\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{3}{4}}\leq\alpha^{t}\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}\right)

To sum up, by combining case (i) and case (ii), the following inequality
holds for
\alpha^{t}>2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}

\alpha^{t+1}\leq\alpha^{t}\left(1-\frac{3}{4}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}\right)

Hence, by using
0<\alpha^{t+1}\leq\alpha^{t}
, we obtain
1\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{t}}{\alpha^{t+1}}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{t}}{\alpha^{t+1}}\right)
and

\displaystyle T=\sum^{T-1}_{t=0}1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum^{T-1}_{t=0}\frac{\alpha^{t}-\alpha^{t+1}}{\frac{3}{4}[\alpha^{t}]^{3}}\leq\frac{4}{3}\times\frac{1}{2}\sum^{T-1}_{t=0}\frac{\alpha^{t}+\alpha^{t+1}}{[\alpha^{t}]^{2}[\alpha^{t+1}]^{2}}(\alpha^{t}-\alpha^{t+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}\sum^{T-1}_{t=0}([\alpha^{t+1}]^{-2}-[\alpha^{t}]^{-2})=\frac{2}{3}([\alpha^{T}]^{-2}-[\alpha^{0}]^{-2})

Namely,

\alpha^{T}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}T+[\alpha^{0}]^{-2}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}T}}

Let’s choose
T=\lceil\frac{1}{6c^{2}}\left[\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\rceil_{+}=\Theta\left(\left[\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, then

\alpha^{T}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}T}}\leq 2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}

Therefore, we show that with
n=\Omega\left(d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\vee\log^{3}\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)
and
|\beta^{0}|=\left\|\pi^{0}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\leq 2c\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}},\alpha^{0}=\|\theta^{0}\|/\sigma\leq 0.1
, if we run finite-sample EM for at most
T=\mathcal{O}\left(\left[\frac{n}{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
iterations, then
\alpha^{T}=\|\theta^{T}\|/\sigma=\mathcal{O}\left(\left[\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)
with probability at least
1-T(\delta+\delta^{\prime})
.

∎

Appendix H
Extension to Finite Low SNR Regime

H.1
Series Expansions for Expectations

In this section, we provide series expansions for several expectations involving the hyperbolic tangent function. These expansions are crucial for analyzing the behavior of EM updates in the low SNR regime.

H.1
Derivative Polynomials for tanh.

Let
P_{n}(t)=\frac{\mathrm{d}^{n}}{\mathrm{d}Z^{n}}\tanh(Z)
for
any
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
t:=\tanh(Z)
, then

P_{0}(t)=\tanh(Z)=t,\quad P_{n+1}(t)=(1-t^{2})\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}P_{n}(t),

and
P_{n}(t)\in\mathbb{Z}[t]
are polynomials in
t
for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and we have:

\deg(P_{n}(t))=n+1,\quad\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}(t)|\leq n\,!\,.

Proof.

Let
P_{n}(t)=\frac{\mathrm{d}^{n}}{\mathrm{d}Z^{n}}\tanh(Z)
for
any
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
t:=\tanh(Z)
, then by the chain rule:

P_{0}(t)=\tanh(Z)=t\in\mathbb{Z}[t],\quad P_{n+1}(t)=\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}Z}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}P_{n}(t)=(1-t^{2})\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}P_{n}(t).

Thus, by the induction, we have
P_{n}(t)\in\mathbb{Z}[t]
are polynomials in
t
for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and we have:

\deg(P_{0}(t))=1,\quad\deg(P_{n+1}(t))=2+[\deg(P_{n}(t))-1]\implies\deg(P_{n}(t))=n+1.

By Bernstein’s inequality (see section 7, page 91 of
Cheney 1966
and
Shadrin 2004
),
we have
\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|\sqrt{1-t^{2}}[\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}p(t)]|\leq\deg(p(t))\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|p(t)|
for any polynomial
p(t)
:

\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|P_{n+1}(t)|\leq\max_{t\in[-1,1]}\left|\sqrt{1-t^{2}}\left[\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}P_{n}(t)\right]\right|\leq\deg(P_{n}(t))\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}(t)|=(n+1)\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}(t)|.

Therefore, by the above recurrence relation, we have:

\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|P_{n}(t)|\leq(n\,!)\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|P_{0}(t)|=(n\,!)\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|t|=n\,!\,.

∎

H.2
Reexpressions of Expectations.

Let
X\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
be a random variable with probability density involving the Bessel function
K_{0}
,
and
\beta=\tanh(\nu)
. Then for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
:
If
0\leq\alpha<1/2
, then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\mathbb{E}[X^{2n}]-\beta(1-\beta^{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)X^{2n}\right]

\displaystyle+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{3}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n+1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\beta^{2})\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X)X^{2n+1}]

\displaystyle+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{3}(\alpha X)X^{2n+1}]

\displaystyle+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{4}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

If
0\leq\alpha<1/4
, then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}[X^{2n}]+(1-\beta^{2})(1-3\beta^{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)X^{2n}\right]

\displaystyle+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{2}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n+1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(1-\beta^{2})\beta\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X)X^{2n+1}]

\displaystyle+2(1-\beta^{2})(-1+2\beta^{2})\beta\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{3}(\alpha X)X^{2n+1}]

\displaystyle+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{3}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

Proof.

We have the following facts for
\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)
and
\beta=\tanh(\nu)
:

\displaystyle\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)+\tanh(-\alpha X+\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\beta-2\beta(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\frac{\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)}{1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\beta-2\beta(1-\beta^{2})\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)

\displaystyle-2\beta(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\beta^{2}\frac{\tanh^{4}(\alpha X)}{1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)},

\displaystyle\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)-\tanh(-\alpha X+\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\frac{\tanh(\alpha X)}{1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\tanh(\alpha X)

\displaystyle+2(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\beta^{2}\tanh^{3}(\alpha X)

\displaystyle+2(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\beta^{4}\cdot\frac{\tanh^{5}(\alpha X)}{1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)},

\displaystyle\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)+\tanh^{2}(-\alpha X+\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\beta^{2}+2(1-\beta^{2})\cdot(1-3\beta^{2})\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)

\displaystyle+2(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\beta^{2}[(1-3\beta^{2})(2-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X))

\displaystyle+(1+\beta^{2})]\frac{\tanh^{4}(\alpha X)}{[1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)]^{2}},

\displaystyle\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)-\tanh^{2}(-\alpha X+\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\beta(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\frac{\tanh(\alpha X)(1-\tanh^{2}(\alpha X))}{[1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)]^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\beta(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\tanh(\alpha X)

\displaystyle+4\beta(1-\beta^{2})\cdot(-1+2\beta^{2})\tanh^{3}(\alpha X)

\displaystyle+4\beta(1-\beta^{2})\cdot\beta^{2}[(-1+2\beta^{2})(2-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X))

\displaystyle-\beta^{2}]\frac{\tanh^{5}(\alpha X)}{[1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)]^{2}}.

Since
X\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
is a symmetric random variable, we have:

\mathbb{E}[f(X)]=\mathbb{E}[f(-X)]=\mathbb{E}[[f(X)+f(-X)]\mathbbm{1}_{X\geq 0}]=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[[f(X)+f(-X)]].

By substituting
f(X)
with
\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n},\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n+1},\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n},\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n+1},n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we only have to consider the upper bounds for the absolute values of the remainder terms in the above equalities.

For the remainder terms of
\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n}]
and
\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n+1}]
,
suppose that for a fixed
\alpha_{0}
and
0\leq\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}<1/2
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\tanh^{4}(\alpha X)}{1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)}X^{2n}\right]\leq\alpha^{2}\mathbb{E}[\sinh^{2}(\alpha X)X^{2n+2}]\leq\alpha^{4}(\mathbb{E}[\sinh^{2}(\alpha_{0}X)X^{2n+2}]/\alpha_{0}^{2})=\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\tanh^{5}(\alpha X)}{1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)}X^{2n+1}\right]\leq\alpha^{3}\mathbb{E}[\sinh^{2}(\alpha X)X^{2n+4}]\leq\alpha^{5}(\mathbb{E}[\sinh^{2}(\alpha_{0}X)X^{2n+4}]/\alpha_{0}^{2})=\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

Note that the coefficients of the remainder for
\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n}]
and
\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2n+1}]
are bounded by:

\displaystyle|(1-3\beta^{2})(2-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X))+(1+\beta^{2})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3,

\displaystyle|(-1+2\beta^{2})(2-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X))-\beta^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2.

Suppose that for a fixed
\alpha_{0}
and
0\leq\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}<1/4
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\tanh^{4}(\alpha X)}{[1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)]^{2}}X^{2n}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[\sinh^{4}(\alpha X)X^{2n}]\leq\alpha^{4}(\mathbb{E}[\sinh^{4}(\alpha_{0}X)X^{2n}]/\alpha_{0}^{4})=\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\tanh^{5}(\alpha X)}{[1-\beta^{2}\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)]^{2}}X^{2n+1}\right]\leq\alpha\mathbb{E}[\sinh^{4}(\alpha X)X^{2n+2}]\leq\alpha^{5}(\mathbb{E}[\sinh^{4}(\alpha_{0}X)X^{2n+2}]/\alpha_{0}^{4})=\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

These remainder terms are bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})
and
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5})
respectively, we have proved the lemma.
∎

H.3
Series Expansions for Expectations of tanh.

Let
X\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
be a random variable with probability density involving the Bessel function
K_{0}
, and
\beta=\tanh(\nu)
. Then for
0\leq\alpha<1/2
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta-9\alpha^{2}\beta(1-\beta^{2})+(1-\beta^{2})\beta\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha(1-\beta^{2})-3\alpha^{3}(1-\beta^{2})(1-3\beta^{2})+(1-\beta^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta-\alpha^{2}\beta(1-\beta^{2})+(1-\beta^{2})\beta\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}).

Proof.

We start by defining these expectations w.r.t the distribution with a density

f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
:

l(\alpha,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}],\quad m(\alpha,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X],\quad n(\alpha,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)].

By Lemma
H.1
and chain rule, and let
Z:=\alpha X+\nu,t=\tanh(Z)=\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)
, then:

\left|\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\alpha^{n}}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)]\right|=\left|\left[\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial Z^{n}}\tanh^{2}(Z)\right]X^{n}\right|=|P_{n}(t)|\cdot\left|X^{n}\right|\leq n\,!\left|X^{n}\right|.

Since
\mathbb{E}|X|^{n}<\infty,\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we can invoke the dominated convergence theorem
to exchange the order of taking limit and taking the expectations
(see Theorem 1.5.8, page 24 of
Durrett 2019
):

\frac{\partial^{n}l(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha^{n}}=\mathbb{E}[P_{n}(t)X^{n+2}],\quad\frac{\partial^{n}m(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha^{n}}=\mathbb{E}[P_{n}(t)X^{n+1}],\quad\frac{\partial^{n}n(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha^{n}}=\mathbb{E}[P_{n}(t)X^{n}].

By using the fact that
P_{n}(t)\mid_{\alpha=0}=P_{n}(\beta)
with
\beta=\tanh(\nu)
, we have:

\left[\frac{\partial^{n}l(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha^{n}}\right]_{\alpha=0}=P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n+2}],\,\left[\frac{\partial^{n}m(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha^{n}}\right]_{\alpha=0}=P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n+1}],\,\left[\frac{\partial^{n}n(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha^{n}}\right]_{\alpha=0}=P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n}].

Since
m(\alpha,\nu)
and
n(\alpha,\nu)
have infinitely many derivatives, we can use the Taylor expansion at
\alpha=0

(see 5.15 Theorem, pages 110-111 of
Rudin 1976
)
to approximate
l(\alpha,\nu),m(\alpha,\nu),n(\alpha,\nu)
:

\displaystyle l(\alpha,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{3}P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n+2}]\frac{\alpha^{n}}{n!}+\mathbb{E}[P_{4}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}}X^{4+2}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}=\sum_{n=0}^{3}P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n+2}]\frac{\alpha^{n}}{n!}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle m(\alpha,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{4}P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n+1}]\frac{\alpha^{n}}{n!}+\mathbb{E}[P_{5}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}}X^{5+1}]\frac{\alpha^{5}}{5!}=\sum_{n=0}^{4}P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n+1}]\frac{\alpha^{n}}{n!}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}),

\displaystyle n(\alpha,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{3}P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n}]\frac{\alpha^{n}}{n!}+\mathbb{E}[P_{4}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime\prime}}X^{4}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}=\sum_{n=0}^{3}P_{n}(\beta)\mathbb{E}[X^{n}]\frac{\alpha^{n}}{n!}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

where
\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime\prime}
are some values between 0 and
\alpha
, and coefficients of remainders are bounded by:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{4}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}}X^{4+2}]/4!\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[X^{4+2}]\right|\cdot 4\,!/4\,!=\mathbb{E}[X^{6}]=(5!!)^{2}=225,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{5}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}}X^{5+1}]/5!\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[X^{5+1}]\right|\cdot 5\,!/5\,!=\mathbb{E}[X^{6}]=(5!!)^{2}=225,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{4}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime\prime}}X^{4}]/4!\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[X^{4}]\right|\cdot 4\,!/4\,!=\mathbb{E}[X^{4}]=(3!!)^{2}=9.

By the recurrence relation
P_{n+1}(t)=(1-t^{2})\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}P_{n}(t),P_{0}(t)=t
in Lemma
H.1
, we have

P_{1}(\beta)=1-\beta^{2},\,P_{2}(\beta)=(1-\beta^{2})\cdot(-2\beta),\,P_{3}(\beta)=(1-\beta^{2})\cdot 2(-1+3\beta^{2}),\,P_{4}(\beta)=(1-\beta^{2})\cdot 8\beta(2-3\beta^{2}).

Note that
\mathbb{E}[X^{2k}]=[(2k-1)!!]^{2},\mathbb{E}[X^{2k-1}]=0
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, then:

\displaystyle l(\alpha,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{0}(\beta)+\frac{9}{2}P_{2}(\beta)\alpha^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})=\beta-9\alpha^{2}\beta(1-\beta^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})

\displaystyle m(\alpha,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{1}(\beta)\alpha+\frac{3}{2}P_{3}(\beta)\alpha^{3}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5})=\alpha(1-\beta^{2})-3\alpha^{3}(1-\beta^{2})(1-3\beta^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5})

\displaystyle n(\alpha,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{0}(\beta)+\frac{1}{2}P_{2}(\beta)\alpha^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})=\beta-\alpha^{2}\beta(1-\beta^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})

By invoking Lemma
H.2
for
0\leq\alpha<1/2
and the fact that
\mathbb{E}[X^{2k}]=[(2k-1)!!]^{2}
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{4}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}}X^{4+2}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}=-\beta(1-\beta^{2})\mathbb{E}[(\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)-(\alpha X)^{2})X^{2}]+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{3}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{5}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}}X^{5+1}]\frac{\alpha^{5}}{5!}=(1-\beta^{2})\mathbb{E}[(\tanh(\alpha X)-[(\alpha X)-(\alpha X)^{3}/3])X]

\displaystyle+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\tanh^{3}(\alpha X)-(\alpha X)^{3})X]+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{4}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{4}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime\prime}}X^{4}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}=-\beta(1-\beta^{2})\mathbb{E}[(\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)-(\alpha X)^{2})]+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{3}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}).

By
t-\frac{t^{3}}{3}\leq\tanh(t)\leq t-\frac{t^{3}}{3}+\frac{2t^{5}}{15},t^{2}-\frac{2t^{4}}{3}\leq\tanh^{2}(t)\leq t^{2},t^{3}-t^{5}\leq\tanh^{3}(t)\leq t^{3},\forall t\geq 0
, we have:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{4}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}}X^{4+2}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}\right|\leq(1-\beta^{2})|\beta|\left(\left|\alpha^{4}\cdot\frac{2}{3}\mathbb{E}[X^{4+2}]\right|+\beta^{2}|\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})|\right)=(1-\beta^{2})|\beta|\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{5}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}}X^{5+1}]\frac{\alpha^{5}}{5!}\right|\leq(1-\beta^{2})\left(\left|\alpha^{5}\cdot\frac{2}{15}\mathbb{E}[X^{5+1}]\right|+\beta^{2}\left|\alpha^{5}\mathbb{E}[X^{5+1}]\right|+\beta^{4}|\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5})|\right)

\displaystyle=(1-\beta^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{4}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime\prime}}X^{4}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}\right|\leq(1-\beta^{2})|\beta|\left(\left|\alpha^{4}\cdot\frac{2}{3}\mathbb{E}[X^{4}]\right|+\beta^{2}|\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})|\right)=(1-\beta^{2})|\beta|\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}).

∎

H.4
Series Expansions for Expectations of tanh Squared.

Let
X\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
be a random variable with probability density involving the Bessel function
K_{0}
, and
\beta=\tanh(\nu)
. Then for
0\leq\alpha<1/4
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta^{2}+9\alpha^{2}(1-\beta^{2})(1-3\beta^{2})+(1-\beta^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\alpha\beta(1-\beta^{2})-12\alpha^{3}\beta(1-\beta^{2})(2-3\beta^{2})+(1-\beta^{2})\beta\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

Proof.

We start by defining these expectations w.r.t the distribution with a density

f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
:

m(\alpha,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X],\quad n(\alpha,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)].

As shown in the proof of Lemma
H.3
, we can invoke the dominated convergence theorem
to exchange the order of taking limit and taking the expectations
(see Theorem 1.5.8, page 24 of
Durrett 2019
):

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\alpha^{n}}m(\alpha,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[P_{n}(t)X^{n+1}],\quad\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\alpha^{n}}n(\alpha,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[P_{n}(t)X^{n}].

When
n=1
, by
P_{1}(t)=1-t^{2}
from Lemma
H.1

and
\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]=1,\mathbb{E}[X]=0
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(1-P_{1}(t))X^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[P_{1}(t)X^{2}]=1-\frac{\partial m(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(1-P_{1}(t))X]=\mathbb{E}[X]-\mathbb{E}[P_{1}(t)X]=-\frac{\partial n(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha}.

Note that
\mathbb{E}[X^{2k}]=[(2k-1)!!]^{2},\mathbb{E}[X^{2k-1}]=0
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and since
m(\alpha,\nu)
and
n(\alpha,\nu)
have infinitely many derivatives, we can use the Taylor expansion at
\alpha=0

(see 5.15 Theorem, pages 110-111 of
Rudin 1976
)
to approximate
\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha}m(\alpha,\nu)
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha}n(\alpha,\nu)
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial m(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{4}P_{n}(\beta)\frac{\mathbb{E}[X^{n+1}]}{(n-1)!}\cdot\alpha^{n-1}+\mathbb{E}[P_{5}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}}X^{5+1}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}=P_{1}(\beta)+\frac{9}{2}P_{3}(\beta)\alpha^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial n(\alpha,\nu)}{\partial\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{5}P_{n}(\beta)\frac{\mathbb{E}[X^{n}]}{(n-1)!}\cdot\alpha^{n-1}+\mathbb{E}[P_{6}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}}X^{6}]\frac{\alpha^{5}}{5!}=P_{2}(\beta)\alpha+\frac{3}{2}P_{4}(\beta)\alpha^{3}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

where
\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}
are some values between 0 and
\alpha
, and the coefficients of remainders are bounded by:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{5}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot X^{5+1}]/4\,!\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X^{6}]\cdot 5\,!/4\,!=5\cdot(5!!)^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{6}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\cdot X^{6}]/5\,!\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X^{6}]\cdot 6\,!/5\,!=6\cdot(5!!)^{2}.

By the recurrence relation
P_{n+1}(t)=(1-t^{2})\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}P_{n}(t)
with
P_{0}(t)=t
from Lemma
H.1
, we have

P_{1}(\beta)=1-\beta^{2},\,P_{2}(\beta)=(1-\beta^{2})\cdot(-2\beta),\,P_{3}(\beta)=(1-\beta^{2})\cdot 2(-1+3\beta^{2}),\,P_{4}(\beta)=(1-\beta^{2})\cdot 8\beta(2-3\beta^{2}).

Hence, by substituting the values of
P_{n}(\beta)
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta^{2}+9\alpha^{2}(1-\beta^{2})(1-3\beta^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\alpha\beta(1-\beta^{2})-12\alpha^{3}\beta(1-\beta^{2})(2-3\beta^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

By invoking Lemma
H.2
for
0\leq\alpha<1/4
and the fact that
\mathbb{E}[X^{2k}]=[(2k-1)!!]^{2}
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[P_{5}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}}X^{5+1}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}=(1-\beta^{2})(1-3\beta^{2})\mathbb{E}[(\tanh^{2}(\alpha X)-(\alpha X)^{2})X^{2}]+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{2}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[P_{6}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}}X^{6}]\frac{\alpha^{5}}{5!}=2(1-\beta^{2})\beta\mathbb{E}[(\tanh(\alpha X)-((\alpha X)-(\alpha X)^{3}/3))X]

\displaystyle+2(1-\beta^{2})(-1+2\beta^{2})\beta\mathbb{E}[(\tanh^{3}(\alpha X)-(\alpha X)^{3})X]+(1-\beta^{2})\beta^{2}\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

By
t-\frac{t^{3}}{3}\leq\tanh(t)\leq t-\frac{t^{3}}{3}+\frac{2t^{5}}{15},t^{2}-\frac{2t^{4}}{3}\leq\tanh^{2}(t)\leq t^{2},t^{3}-t^{5}\leq\tanh^{3}(t)\leq t^{3},\forall t\geq 0
, we have:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{5}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}}X^{5+1}]\frac{\alpha^{4}}{4!}\right|\leq(1-\beta^{2})\left(\left|\alpha^{4}\cdot 2\cdot\frac{2}{3}\mathbb{E}[X^{4+2}]\right|+\beta^{2}|\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4})|\right)=(1-\beta^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{4}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[P_{6}(t)\mid_{\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}}X^{6}]\frac{\alpha^{5}}{5!}\right|\leq(1-\beta^{2})|\beta|\left(\left|\alpha^{5}\cdot 2\cdot\frac{2}{15}\mathbb{E}[X^{5+1}]\right|+\left|\alpha^{5}\cdot 2\cdot\mathbb{E}[X^{5+1}]\right|+\beta^{2}|\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5})|\right)

\displaystyle=(1-\beta^{2})|\beta|\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{5}).

∎

H.5
Expectations w.r.t Gaussian Distribution.

Let
X\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
be a random variable with probability density involving the Bessel function
K_{0}
,
and
Z_{1},Z_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{2}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}^{3}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{1}Z_{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{2}^{2}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)],

\displaystyle-\alpha\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}],

where
\tanh^{\prime}(\cdot)
is the derivative of
\tanh(\cdot)
.

Proof.

For
\alpha=0
,
We can justify the above identities by
\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[Z_{2}^{2}]=1,\mathbb{E}[X]=\mathbb{E}[Z_{1}Z_{2}]=0
,
therefore, we only need to prove the identities for
\alpha\neq 0
.
By applying Stein’s lemma (see Lemma 2.1 of
Ross 2011
) with respect to
Z_{1}
,
and noting the fact that
X:=Z_{1}Z_{2}\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
for any
Z_{1},Z_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

(see page 50, section 4.4 of chapter 12 in
Johnson et al. 1970
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\ln\cosh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{2}}{\partial Z_{1}}\right]=\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{E}[\ln\cosh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{2}\times Z_{1}],

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{E}[\ln\cosh(\alpha X+\nu)X],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{2}}{\partial Z_{1}}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{2}\times Z_{1}],

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}^{3}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{1}Z_{2}^{2}}{\partial Z_{1}}-\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{2}^{2}\right],

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}]-\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{E}[\ln\cosh(\alpha X+\nu)X].

By combining the above equations, the first two identities are proved.
For the last two identities, we note that
\mathbb{E}[X]=0
,
\tanh^{\prime}(\cdot)=1-\tanh^{2}(\cdot)
, then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\alpha\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)].

∎

H.2
Approximations of EM Update Rules in Finite Low SNR Regime

H.6
Approximations of EM Update Rules in Finite Low SNR Regime.

In the finite low SNR regime where
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\lesssim 1
, the EM update rules are:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\left[\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X]+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}\left[\eta\beta^{\ast}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)]-\alpha\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X]\right)+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)\right],

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)]+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}),

where
X\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
is a random variable with probability density involving the Bessel function
K_{0}
,

\beta^{\ast}=\tanh\nu^{\ast}=\pi^{\ast}(1)-\pi^{\ast}(2)
,

\rho=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
is the cosine of angle between
\theta
and
\theta^{\ast}
,

\vec{e}_{1}=\theta/\|\theta\|
and
\vec{e}_{2}=\frac{\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}}{\|\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}\|}
form an orthonormal basis,
and
\alpha=\|\theta\|/\sigma,\beta=\tanh\nu=\pi(1)-\pi(2)
.

Proof.

We start by viewing the response variable
y=\varepsilon+\Delta\varepsilon
as pure noise
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
with some small perturbation
\Delta\varepsilon:=(-1)^{z+1}\langle\theta^{\ast},x\rangle
, where
x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.
To simplify our analysis, we introduce several notations.
First, let
\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
denote the cosine of angle between
\theta
and
\theta^{\ast}
. Next, we define a pair of orthonormal vectors:

\vec{e}_{1}:=\theta/\|\theta\|
and
\vec{e}_{2}:=\frac{\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}}{\|\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}\|}
.
For the noise terms, we introduce three independent standard Gaussians:
Z_{1}:=\varepsilon/\sigma\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
Z_{2}:=\langle x,\vec{e}_{1}\rangle=\langle x,\theta\rangle/\|\theta\|\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),Z_{3}:=\langle x,\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
Finally, for the low SNR regime where
\eta:=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\lesssim 1
, we define
\alpha:=\|\theta\|/\sigma
and
\beta:=\tanh\nu=\pi(1)-\pi(2)
.
Then, we can express
\Delta Z_{1}:=\Delta\varepsilon/\sigma
as:

\Delta Z_{1}=\Delta\varepsilon/\sigma=(-1)^{z+1}\langle\theta^{\ast},x\rangle/\sigma=(-1)^{z+1}\eta(\rho Z_{2}+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{3})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\eta^{2})

Then, we can define
F,G
as the following functions:

F(Z):=\tanh(\alpha Z_{2}Z+\nu)Z,\quad G(Z):=\tanh(\alpha Z_{2}Z+\nu).

Consequently, we can express the EM update rules
M(\theta,\nu),N(\theta,\nu)
at population level as:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)yx\right]/\sigma=\mathbb{E}[F(Z_{1}+\Delta Z_{1})(\vec{e}_{1}Z_{2}+\vec{e}_{2}Z_{3})],

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}[G(Z_{1}+\Delta Z_{1})].

By introducing the derivative of tanh in Lemma
H.1
and invoking Taylor’s theorem with remainder (see 5.15 Theorem, pages 110-111 of
Rudin 1976
), we have:

\displaystyle F(Z_{1}+\Delta Z_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F(Z_{1})+F^{\prime}(Z_{1})\Delta Z_{1}+\frac{1}{2}F^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\xi\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2},

\displaystyle G(Z_{1}+\Delta Z_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G(Z_{1})+G^{\prime}(Z_{1})\Delta Z_{1}+\frac{1}{2}G^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\zeta\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2},

where
\xi,\zeta\in(0,1)
, remainders are bounded by (use
\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|P_{2}(t)|\leq 2,\max_{t\in[-1,1]}|P_{1}(t)|\leq 1
):

\displaystyle|F^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\xi\Delta Z_{1})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|(\alpha Z_{2})^{2}(Z_{1}+\xi\Delta Z_{1})P_{2}(G(Z_{1}+\xi\Delta Z_{1}))+2(\alpha Z_{2})P_{1}(G(Z_{1}+\xi\Delta Z_{1}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\alpha^{2}Z_{2}^{2}(|Z_{1}|+|\Delta Z_{1}|)+2\alpha|Z_{2}|,

\displaystyle|G^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\zeta\Delta Z_{1})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|(\alpha Z_{2})^{2}P_{2}(G(Z_{1}+\zeta\Delta Z_{1}))|\leq 2\alpha^{2}Z_{2}^{2}.

and by the orthogonality of
\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{2}

and
|\Delta Z_{1}|^{3}\leq 4\eta^{3}(|Z_{2}|^{3}+|Z_{3}|^{3}),(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}\leq 2\eta^{2}(|Z_{2}|^{2}+|Z_{3}|^{2})
:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left\langle\frac{1}{2}F^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\xi\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}(\vec{e}_{1}Z_{2}+\vec{e}_{2}Z_{3}),\vec{e}_{1}\right\rangle\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left|F^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\xi\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}Z_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[[\alpha^{2}Z_{2}^{2}(|Z_{1}|+|\Delta Z_{1}|)+\alpha|Z_{2}|](\Delta Z_{1})^{2}|Z_{2}|]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\alpha^{2}|Z_{1}||Z_{2}|^{3}+\alpha|Z_{2}|^{2})(|Z_{2}|^{2}+|Z_{3}|^{2})]+4\eta^{3}\alpha^{2}\mathbb{E}[|Z_{2}|^{3}(|Z_{2}|^{3}+|Z_{3}|^{3})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\eta^{2}\alpha(1+\frac{5}{\pi}\alpha)+4\cdot(15+\frac{8}{\pi})\eta^{3}\alpha^{2}=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha),

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left\langle\frac{1}{2}F^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\zeta\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}(\vec{e}_{1}Z_{2}+\vec{e}_{2}Z_{3}),\vec{e}_{2}\right\rangle\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left|F^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\zeta\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}Z_{3}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[[\alpha^{2}Z_{2}^{2}(|Z_{1}|+|\Delta Z_{1}|)+\alpha|Z_{2}|](\Delta Z_{1})^{2}|Z_{3}|]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\alpha^{2}|Z_{1}||Z_{2}|^{2}|Z_{3}|+\alpha|Z_{2}||Z_{3}|)(|Z_{2}|^{2}+|Z_{3}|^{2})]+4\eta^{3}\alpha^{2}\mathbb{E}[|Z_{2}|^{2}|Z_{3}|(|Z_{2}|^{3}+|Z_{3}|^{3})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{16}{\pi}\eta^{2}\alpha(1+\frac{5}{4}\alpha)+4\cdot(3+\frac{16}{\pi})\eta^{3}\alpha^{2}=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha),

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}G^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\zeta\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}\right]\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left|G^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\zeta\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\alpha^{2}Z_{2}^{2}(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}\mathbb{E}[|Z_{2}|^{2}(|Z_{2}|^{2}+|Z_{3}|^{2})]=8\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}).

Note
\mathbb{E}[Z_{3}]=0,\mathbb{E}[Z_{3}^{2}]=1
and
Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3}
are independent and
\mathbb{E}[(-1)^{z+1}]=\beta^{\ast}=\tanh\nu^{\ast}
, then:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(F(Z_{1})+F(Z_{1})\Delta Z_{1})(\vec{e}_{1}Z_{2}+\vec{e}_{2}Z_{3})]+\vec{e}_{1}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)+\vec{e}_{2}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\left[\mathbb{E}[F(Z_{1})Z_{2}]+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho\mathbb{E}[F^{\prime}(Z_{1})Z_{2}^{2}]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}\left[\eta\beta^{\ast}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\mathbb{E}[F^{\prime}(Z_{1})]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)\right],

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G(Z_{1})+G^{\prime}(Z_{1})\Delta Z_{1}]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G(Z_{1})]+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho\mathbb{E}[G^{\prime}(Z_{1})Z_{2}]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}).

Let
X:=Z_{1}Z_{2}\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
, by invoking Lemma
H.5
for expectations w.r.t Gaussians, then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F(Z_{1})Z_{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{1}Z_{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F^{\prime}(Z_{1})Z_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)Z_{2}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}^{3}]=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F^{\prime}(Z_{1})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)]+\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)]-\alpha\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G(Z_{1})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)]=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G^{\prime}(Z_{1})Z_{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{\prime}(\alpha Z_{1}Z_{2}+\nu)\alpha Z_{2}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X].

Substituting the above results into the EM update rules, we have:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\left[\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X]+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X^{2}]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}\left[\eta\beta^{\ast}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)]-\alpha\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha X+\nu)X]\right)+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha)\right],

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)]+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha X+\nu)X]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}).

Therefore, we have established exact expressions of the EM update rules
M(\theta,\nu)
and
N(\theta,\nu)
for the regression parameters
\theta
and the mixing weight imbalance
\tanh\nu=\pi(1)-\pi(2)

in the low SNR regime where
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\lesssim 1
.
These expressions involve expectations with respect to the random variable
X\sim f_{X}(x)=\frac{K_{0}(|x|)}{\pi}
,
where
K_{0}
is the modified Bessel function of the second kind.
∎

.

When
|\rho|=1
,
(\Delta Z_{1})_{|\rho|=1}=(-1)^{z+1}\eta Z_{2}
is independent of
Z_{3}
, the remainder satisfies:

\mathbb{E}\left\langle\frac{1}{2}F^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\xi\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}(\vec{e}_{1}Z_{2}+\vec{e}_{2}Z_{3}),\vec{e}_{2}\right\rangle_{|\rho|=1}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}F^{\prime\prime}(Z_{1}+\Delta Z_{1})(\Delta Z_{1})^{2}\right]_{|\rho|=1}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[Z_{3}]}_{=0}=0.

H.3
Approximate Dynamic Equations in Finite Low SNR Regime

H.7
.

For the EM iterations of
\alpha^{t}:=\|\theta^{t}\|/\sigma,\beta^{t}:=\tanh\nu^{t},\rho^{t}:=\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle/\|\theta^{t}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|
,
given
\alpha^{t}<1/4
and
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\lesssim 1
in low SNR regime, we have the approximate equations for
\alpha^{t+1},\beta^{t+1}
:

\displaystyle\alpha^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X]+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X^{2}]+\eta^{2}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{[\beta^{\ast}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2}/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\vee[\alpha^{t}]^{2}}{\alpha^{t}}\right),

\displaystyle\beta^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})]+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X]+\eta^{2}\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2}).

Furthermore, with the notations
C_{\eta}=\alpha^{t}\frac{(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{|\beta^{t}\cdot\beta^{\ast}|},C^{\prime}_{\eta}=\sqrt{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}
, when
\eta\lesssim C_{\eta}\wedge C^{\prime}_{\eta}
, we have:

\displaystyle\rho^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{t}+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\eta\beta^{\ast}(\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})]-\alpha^{t}\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X])/\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+\rho^{t}\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{3}\right),

where
X
is a random variable with the density function
X\sim f_{X}(x)=K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
involving the Bessel function
K_{0}
.

Proof.

To simplify the notations, we let
\alpha^{t},\beta^{t},\rho^{t},\vec{e}_{1}^{t},\vec{e}_{2}^{t}
denote
\alpha,\beta,\rho,\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{2}
at iteration
t
.
We start by using Lemma
H.3
and Lemma
H.4
, we define
s^{t}:=[\alpha^{t}]^{2}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})
, then:

\displaystyle I_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})]=\beta^{t}(1-s^{t}[1+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2})]),

\displaystyle I_{1}^{t}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X]=(s^{t}/\alpha^{t})[1-3[\alpha^{t}]^{2}(1-3[\beta^{t}]^{2})+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{4})],

\displaystyle I_{2}^{t}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X^{2}]=\beta^{t}(1-9s^{t}[1+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2})]),

\displaystyle J^{t}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})]-\alpha^{t}\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X]=\beta^{t}(1-3s^{t}[1+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2})]).

In the low SNR regime where
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\lesssim 1
,
by invoking the proven Theorem in Subsection
H.2
, we have:

\displaystyle M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})/\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}^{t}\left[I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{t})\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}^{t}\left[\eta\beta^{\ast}\sqrt{1-[\rho^{t}]^{2}}J^{t}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{t})\right],

\displaystyle N(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{0}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{1}^{t}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}).

For
\alpha^{t+1}=\|M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})\|/\sigma,\beta^{t+1}=N(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})
, by applying the triangle inequality, we have:

\displaystyle\alpha^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{(I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t})^{2}+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})(\eta\beta^{\ast}J^{t})^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{t}),

\displaystyle\beta^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{0}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{1}^{t}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}).

The cosine
\rho^{t+1}
of angle between
\theta^{t+1}
and
\theta^{\ast}
is determined by the inner product of

M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})/\sigma
and
\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|=\rho^{t}\vec{e}_{1}^{t}+\sqrt{1-[\rho^{t}]^{2}}\vec{e}_{2}^{t}
:

\rho^{t+1}\alpha^{t+1}=\left\langle M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})/\sigma,\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\right\rangle=\rho^{t}I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}[\rho^{t}]^{2}I_{2}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})J^{t}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{t}).

To derive the approximation for
\alpha^{t+1}
when
\eta
is small enough, we first note that following inequality:

r+\epsilon\cos\gamma\leq\sqrt{r^{2}+\epsilon^{2}+2r\epsilon\cos\gamma}\leq r+\epsilon\cos\gamma+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2r}=r+\epsilon\cos\gamma+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r}\right).

By letting
r=I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}(I_{2}^{t}-J^{t}),\epsilon=\eta\beta^{\ast}J^{t},\cos\gamma=\rho^{t}
, we have:

\alpha^{t+1}=I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t}+\eta^{2}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{[\beta^{\ast}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2}/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\vee[\alpha^{t}]^{2}}{\alpha^{t}}\right).

where
I_{2}^{t}-J^{t}=-6\beta^{t}s^{t}[1+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2})]
,
and
r=\mathcal{O}(s^{t}/\alpha^{t})=(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{t})
when
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}(1)\leq 1/(\alpha^{t}|\beta^{t}\rho^{t}\beta^{\ast}|)
,
namely
I_{1}\gtrsim\eta|\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}(I_{2}^{t}-J^{t})|
.

Furthurmore, when
\frac{\eta}{\alpha^{t}}\lesssim\frac{(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{|\beta^{t}\cdot\beta^{\ast}|}
, we have
I_{1}^{t}\gtrsim\eta|\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t}|\vee\eta^{2}[\beta^{\ast}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2}/(\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}))
;
when
\eta\lesssim\sqrt{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}
, we have
I_{1}^{t}\gtrsim\eta^{2}\alpha^{t}
.
Therefore, if
\eta\lesssim\min\left(\alpha^{t}\frac{(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{|\beta^{t}\cdot\beta^{\ast}|},\sqrt{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}\right)=\alpha^{t}\frac{(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{|\beta^{t}\cdot\beta^{\ast}|}\wedge\sqrt{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}
, we have

I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t}=\mathcal{O}(I_{1}^{t})=\mathcal{O}(s^{t}/\alpha^{t})=(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{t}),\quad\alpha^{t+1}=\mathcal{O}(I_{1}^{t})=\mathcal{O}(s^{t}/\alpha^{t})=(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{t}).

For the simplicity of notations, we define
C_{\eta}:=\alpha^{t}\frac{(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{|\beta^{t}\cdot\beta^{\ast}|},C^{\prime}_{\eta}:=\sqrt{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}
,
then our assumptions on
\eta
can be rewritten as
\eta\lesssim C_{\eta}\wedge C^{\prime}_{\eta}
.
By using such fact
1/(1+x)=1+\mathcal{O}(|x|),\forall x\in[-1/2,1/2]
and
I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t}=(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{t})
, then:

(I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t})/\alpha^{t+1}=1+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\vee\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right),

Similary, by letting
x=(\alpha^{t+1}-I_{1}^{t})/I_{1}^{t}
and note that
I_{1}^{t}=(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{t}),I_{2}^{t}=\mathcal{O}(\beta^{t}),J^{t}=\mathcal{O}(|\beta^{t}|)
, then:

1/\alpha^{t+1}=\frac{1}{I_{1}^{t}}\left[1+\rho^{t}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\vee\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)\right],

\eta\beta^{\ast}J^{t}/\alpha^{t+1}=\eta\beta^{\ast}J^{t}/I_{1}^{t}+\rho^{t}\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)\left[\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\vee\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right]\right).

Note that
\alpha^{t+1}=\mathcal{O}(I_{1}^{t})=\mathcal{O}(s^{t}/\alpha^{t})=(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{t})
, therefore we have:

\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{t})/\alpha^{t+1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right).

Note the following relation that we obtained earlier:

\rho^{t+1}=\rho^{t}\cdot(I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t})/\alpha^{t+1}+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot(\eta\beta^{\ast}J^{t}/\alpha^{t+1})+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{t})/\alpha^{t+1},

by combining the above results and invoking the assumption
\eta\lesssim C_{\eta}\wedge C^{\prime}_{\eta}
, then we have:

\rho^{t+1}=\rho^{t}+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\eta\beta^{\ast}J^{t}/I_{1}^{t}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+\rho^{t}\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{3}\right).

In summary, with the notations

C_{\eta}=\alpha^{t}\frac{(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{|\beta^{t}\cdot\beta^{\ast}|},C^{\prime}_{\eta}=\sqrt{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}
,
given
\alpha^{t}<1/4,\eta\lesssim 1
, we have:

\displaystyle\alpha^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t}+\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\vee\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\beta^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{0}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{1}^{t}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}[\alpha^{t}]^{2}),

with the assumption
\eta\lesssim C_{\eta}\wedge C^{\prime}_{\eta}
, we also have:

\rho^{t+1}=\rho^{t}+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\eta\beta^{\ast}J^{t}/I_{1}^{t}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+\rho^{t}\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{3}\right).

∎

.

When
|\rho^{t}|=1
, by noting the remark in Subsection
H.2
,
the remainder term of the EM update rules in the direction of
\vec{e}_{2}^{t}
is exactly zero,
namely
[\vec{e}_{2}^{t}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{t})]_{|\rho^{t}|=1}=\vec{0}
, thus we have:

\left[M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})/\sigma\right]_{|\rho^{t}|=1}=\vec{e}_{1}^{t}\left[I_{1}^{t}+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}I_{2}^{t}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\alpha^{t})\right].

Therefore, we have
|\rho^{t+1}|=1
if
|\rho^{t}|=1

since
\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|=\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{t})\vec{e}_{1}^{t}
when
|\rho^{t}|=1
and

|\rho^{t+1}|=|\langle M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})/\|M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})\|,\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\rangle|=|\langle\vec{e}_{1}^{t},\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{t})\vec{e}_{1}^{t}\rangle|=|\langle\vec{e}_{1}^{t},\vec{e}_{1}^{t}\rangle|=1.

H.8
Dynamic Equations of EM Update Rules in Low SNR Regime.

For the EM iterations of
\alpha^{t}:=\|\theta^{t}\|/\sigma,\beta^{t}:=\tanh\nu^{t},\rho^{t}:=\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle/\|\theta^{t}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|
,
given
\alpha^{t}<1/4
and
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\lesssim 1
in low SNR regime, we have the approximate dynamic equations for
\alpha^{t+1},\beta^{t+1}
:

\displaystyle\alpha^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{2}])+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}\beta^{t}(1-9(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})[\alpha^{t}]^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-[\beta^{2}])\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})+\eta^{2}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{[\beta^{\ast}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2}/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\vee[\alpha^{t}]^{2}}{\alpha^{t}}\right),

\displaystyle\beta^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta^{t}(1-(1-[\beta^{2}])[\alpha^{t}]^{2})+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})+(1-[\beta^{2}])\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})+\eta^{2}\mathcal{O}\left([\alpha^{t}]^{2}\right)

Furthermore, with the notations
C_{\eta}=\alpha^{t}\frac{(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{|\beta^{t}\cdot\beta^{\ast}|},C^{\prime}_{\eta}=\sqrt{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}
, when
\eta\lesssim C_{\eta}\wedge C^{\prime}_{\eta}
, we have:

\displaystyle\rho^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{t}+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\eta\beta^{\ast}\frac{\beta^{t}(1-6[\alpha^{t}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\eta\beta^{\ast}\beta^{t}(1/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})+1)\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+\rho^{t}\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{3}\right),

Proof.

By using Lemma
H.3
and Lemma
H.4
and defining
s^{t}:=[\alpha^{t}]^{2}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})
, we have:

\displaystyle I_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})]=\beta^{t}(1-s^{t}[1+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2})]),

\displaystyle I_{1}^{t}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X]=\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})[1-3[\alpha^{t}]^{2}(1-3[\beta^{t}]^{2})+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{4})],

\displaystyle I_{2}^{t}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X^{2}]=\beta^{t}(1-9s^{t}[1+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2})]),

\displaystyle J^{t}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})]-\alpha^{t}\mathbb{E}[\tanh^{2}(\alpha^{t}X+\nu^{t})X]=\beta^{t}(1-3s^{t}[1+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2})]).

By substituting the above results into the approximate dynamic equations in proven Theorem
H.7
,
note that
(1-[\beta^{2}])\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}\beta^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{4})=(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})
, we have:

\displaystyle\alpha^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{2}])+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}\beta^{t}(1-9(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})[\alpha^{t}]^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-[\beta^{2}])\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})+\eta^{2}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{[\beta^{\ast}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2}/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\vee[\alpha^{t}]^{2}}{\alpha^{t}}\right).

Note that
\beta^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{4})+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})=(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})
,
we have:

\displaystyle\beta^{t+1}=\beta^{t}(1-(1-[\beta^{2}])[\alpha^{t}]^{2})+\eta\beta^{\ast}\rho^{t}\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})+(1-[\beta^{2}])\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})+\eta^{2}\mathcal{O}\left([\alpha^{t}]^{2}\right).

Furthermore, with the notations
C_{\eta}=\alpha^{t}\frac{(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{|\beta^{t}\cdot\beta^{\ast}|},C^{\prime}_{\eta}=\sqrt{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}
, when
\eta\lesssim C_{\eta}\wedge C^{\prime}_{\eta}
, we have:

\displaystyle J^{t}/I_{1}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{t}/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{\alpha^{t}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-3[\alpha^{t}]^{2}(1-3[\beta^{t}]^{2})+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{4})}-3\alpha^{t}\beta^{t}\frac{1+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{2})}{1-3[\alpha^{t}]^{2}(1-3[\beta^{t}]^{2})+\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{4})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\beta^{t}/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{\alpha^{t}}+3\frac{\alpha^{t}\beta^{t}}{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}-9\frac{\alpha^{t}[\beta^{t}]^{3}}{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}+\frac{\beta^{t}}{1-[\beta^{t}]^{2}}\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})\right)-3\alpha^{t}\beta^{t}+\beta^{t}\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{t}(1-6[\alpha^{t}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}+\beta^{t}(1/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})+1)\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3}),

hence,

\displaystyle\rho^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{t}+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\eta\beta^{\ast}\frac{\beta^{t}(1-6[\alpha^{t}]^{2}[\beta^{t}]^{2})}{\alpha^{t}(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\eta\beta^{\ast}\beta^{t}(1/(1-[\beta^{t}]^{2})+1)\mathcal{O}([\alpha^{t}]^{3})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C^{\prime}_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+\rho^{t}\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{2}\right)+(1-[\rho^{t}]^{2})\cdot\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{C_{\eta}}\right)^{3}\right).
